Texts: Aschenbrenner, Situational Awareness (2024); Narayanan and Kapoor, AI scaling myths (2024).

- `rd-reply-narayanan-kapoor-2024-ai-scaling-myths-to-aschenbrenner-2024-situational-awareness`: Narayanan and Kapoor's post of June 2024 addresses Aschenbrenner's essay of 2024, which it names, in its further-reading note.
  > A recent essay by Leopold Aschenbrenner made waves due to its claim that “AGI by 2027 is strikingly plausible”. We haven’t tried to give a point-by-point rebuttal here — Section "Further reading"
  > His arguments for his timeline are entertaining and thought provoking, but fundamentally an exercise in trendline extrapolation. — Section "Further reading"

### Hanson–Yudkowsky AI-foom debate and later posts, 2008–2022

2008-12 · Robin Hanson, Eliezer Yudkowsky

The debate of 2008–2013 is in the dataset and is linked, not re-recorded. The pack adds later posts by the two participants: Hanson (2014, 2022) and Yudkowsky (2017), which names 'my and Robin Hanson’s AI-foom debate'. No reply item is made for Yudkowsky's post because it names the debate rather than one text; Hanson's reply to it on LessWrong, linked from the MIRI page as 'Reply from Robin Hanson', could not be retrieved (HTTP 403).

Texts: Hanson, AI Go Foom (2008); Yudkowsky, Hard Takeoff (2008); Hanson, What Core Argument? (2008); Hanson, Debating Yudkowsky (2011); Hanson, Foom Debate, Again (2013); Hanson, I Still Don't Get Foom (2014); Yudkowsky, AlphaGo Zero and the Foom Debate (2017); Hanson, Foom Update (2022).

### Christiano and Yudkowsky on takeoff speeds, 2018–2022

2018-02 · Paul Christiano, Eliezer Yudkowsky

In the dataset (A4, Y11) and linked; the pack adds only a record of Christiano (2018) on R16.

Texts: Christiano, Takeoff speeds (2018); Yudkowsky, Yudkowsky and Christiano discuss "Takeoff Speeds" (2021); Christiano, Where I agree and disagree with Eliezer (2022).

## Search log

- **R07** (catastrophe-and-priorities). Seed texts read: Bostrom 2002 (JET HTML; author's PDF compared for version only), Hawking–Russell–Tegmark–Wilczek 2014, Altman 2015, CAIS statement 2023, Ng 2023 (two letters), Andreessen 2023, Bengio et al. 2023, Carlsmith 2022. arXiv API: abs:"existential risk" AND abs:AI, and abs:"catastrophic risk" AND abs:AI, sorted by submission date ascending, first 50 results each read by title (abstracts for three). Web search for discovery only (Ng letters, International AI Safety Report address, Senate hearing records). _Earliest:_ Bostrom (March 2002) is the earliest text found in the period that names AI (a badly programmed superintelligence) as an extinction risk and argues that existential risks merit very serious attention; an earlier version was presented in April 2001 but was not retrieved. Before it, the dataset holds Yudkowsky's SL4 messages and Creating Friendly AI (2001) on overlapping Y-codes, and Vinge (1993) and Good (1965) before the period; they are not re-recorded. The arXiv search returned nothing earlier than 2014 on these terms. Earliest contesting located: Pinker's Edge response of 2014, found by the same-family audit among the pack's sources, ahead of Hawkins (2 March 2015); Ng (7 June 2023) was the earliest found by the group's own search.
- **R08** (catastrophe-and-priorities). Seed texts read: Bostrom 2002, Altman 2015, Dafoe and Russell 2016, Carlsmith 2022, FLI letter 2023, Bengio 2023 (rogue AIs), Hendrycks et al. 2023, Bengio et al. 2023, Russell's oral statement of 25 July 2023 (read, not recorded), Hinton 2024, International AI Safety Report 2025; contesting texts by Andreessen 2023, Richards et al. 2023, Gans 2023, Narayanan and Kapoor 2025, and Sotos 2021 (found by the arXiv search). arXiv API: abs:"loss of control" AND abs:AI, ascending, first 50 by title. _Earliest:_ Bostrom (March 2002) section 4.4 is the earliest text found in the period stating that a superintelligence given mistaken goals could annihilate humankind. The dataset's Yudkowsky texts of 2000–2001 (SL4, Creating Friendly AI) on Y19/Y20 and his 2006 chapter precede or accompany it and are linked, not re-recorded; the treacherous-turn (B1) and deceptive-alignment (B3) records belong to the misalignment group. Earliest contesting located: Pinker's Edge response of 2014, found by the same-family audit among the pack's sources; Hawkins (2 March 2015) and the 2019 thread are also earlier than Sotos (August 2021), the earliest found by the group's own search.
- **R09** (catastrophe-and-priorities). Seed texts read: Bostrom 2002, Brundage et al. 2018 (read; recorded only in this log), Marcus 2023, Bengio 2023, Hendrycks et al. 2023, Amodei's oral Senate statement of 25 July 2023 (printed hearing record), Hinton 2024 (lists bioweapons as a short-term risk), Sotos 2021; contesting: RAND red-team study 2024. arXiv API: abs:"malicious use" AND abs:AI, ascending, first 44 results by title. OpenAI's January 2024 biological early-warning study could not be retrieved (HTTP 403). _Earliest:_ Bostrom (March 2002) section 6.3, on a small group in control of the first superintelligence selecting its goals, is the earliest text found in the period that names deliberate use of advanced AI to entrench power with an existential outcome; it names no weapon. The earliest text found that names AI-enabled weapons or attacks at mass-casualty scale is Marcus (28 March 2023); Brundage et al. (February 2018) treats malicious use at length but limits itself to digital, physical and political security threats without a catastrophe-scale claim and was therefore not recorded. Earliest contesting located: Pinker's Edge response of 2014 (paraphrased), found by the same-family audit among the pack's sources; the RAND red-team study (2024) is recorded as bearing on the proposition, since it reports no measurable uplift from the models tested without disputing the proposition.
- **R11** (catastrophe-and-priorities). Seed texts read: Etzioni 2016, Andreessen 2023, Ng 2023 (October letter), Narayanan and Kapoor 2024 and 2025, DAIR 2023; Brooks 2014 found by web search for his texts. Edge "Myth of AI" responses (2014) and Pinker belong to the goals-and-drives group and were not read here. arXiv API: abs:"existential risk" AND abs:harms, ascending, 15 results by title; Kovarik et al. 2024 ("Extinction Risks from AI: Invisible to Science?") read by abstract, not recorded. _Earliest:_ Brooks (Robohub repost of 11 November 2014 of a Rethink Robotics post) is the earliest text found that calls worry about malevolent AI fear mongering and a waste of time. The dataset's Yudkowsky texts on Y20 (2001–2007) contest a narrower film-fiction version of the claim and are linked from the Brooks record. Earliest contesting located: Russell's Edge response of 2014, which answers Brooks, found by the same-family audit among the pack's sources; Dafoe and Russell (November 2016) was the earliest found by the group's own search.
- **R12** (catastrophe-and-priorities). Seed texts read: DAIR 2023, Gebru and Torres 2024, Marcus 2023, Bengio FAQ 2023 and 2024, Richards et al. 2023, Nature editorial 2023, Bender and Hanna 2023; Crawford and Calo 2016 and Hoes and Gilardi 2025 found by web search. Crawford's New York Times essay of June 2016 returned HTTP 403. Swoboda et al. 2025 ("Examining Popular Arguments Against AI Existential Risk", arXiv) read by abstract and not recorded, to keep the group's balance. _Earliest:_ Crawford and Calo (Nature, 13 October 2016) is the earliest text found, but only the journal's standfirst could be read, so it is recorded as context (a relay) without a role. Zador's comment in the 2019 thread is recorded as context, since it names no documented present harms of AI systems. The earliest text found whose authors' own wording meets the criterion is the DAIR statement (31 March 2023). Earliest contesting located: Marcus (28 March 2023).
- **balance** (catastrophe-and-priorities). Count by position after drafting: 23 records argue for the risk propositions (articulating R07–R09 or contesting R11–R12), 19 argue the counter-propositions (articulating R11–R12 or contesting R07–R09), and 2 bear on R08 without a side (FLI letter, International AI Safety Report). _Earliest:_ The searches found more developed texts on the risk side for R08 and R09 (papers, a consensus paper, testimony) and fewer primary contesting texts on R07 and R09: most published contestations of R07 are interviews, social media posts or debate recordings without transcripts, which the scope excludes. Not padded.
- **debates** (debates). The LessWrong copy of the October 2019 Facebook thread, which the goals-and-drives pass could not retrieve (HTTP 403 on the page), was retrieved by the integrator through the LessWrong GraphQL API on 2026-10-29. _Earliest:_ No earliest-located role is assigned from this thread; its records are dated 2019-10-04 by the copy.
- **R01** (goals-and-drives). Seed texts read: Bostrom 2003 (author's revised HTML and PDF), Bostrom 2012 (author's PDF), Russell 2014 (Edge) and 2019 (IEEE Spectrum), the Edge 'Myth of AI' responses of 2014 (Pinker, Brooks, Kelly, Russell read in full; others skimmed), Pinker 2015 (Edge) and 2018 (Popular Science), Zador and LeCun 2019, Brooks 2017, Hawkins 2015, Andreessen 2023, Bengio 2024. arXiv API, abs:"orthogonality thesis" and abs:orthogonality AND abs:superintelligence, sorted by submission date ascending, all results read by title and abstract (6 and 1). Web search for discovery of Müller and Cannon (2021) and its reply by Häggström (2021), and of Armstrong (2013). _Earliest:_ Bostrom 2012 is the earliest located text with an attested date that meets the criterion. Bostrom's 2003 paper states the proposition but its wording survives only in an undated revised version, so it is recorded as an articulation without the role. The dataset already records Yudkowsky texts contesting the contrary claim Y02 from 13 November 2000 (SL4), 2004, 2008 and 2015 (Arbital, 'Orthogonality Thesis'); they precede Bostrom 2012 and are linked, not re-recorded. Earliest contesting located: Goertzel (November 2015), found by the same-family audit among the pack's sources; Brooks (September 2017) was the earliest found by the group's own search. Pinker (2018) is recorded on R06 and R10a instead.
- **R02** (goals-and-drives). Seed texts read: Omohundro 2007 (revised 2008) and 2008, Bostrom 2003 and 2012, Russell 2014 and 2019, Turner 2019 (arXiv v1), Zador and LeCun 2019, Pinker 2014, 2015 and 2018, Kelly 2014, Andreessen 2023, Bengio 2024. arXiv API, abs:"instrumental convergence" (15), abs:"convergent instrumental" (5), abs:"power-seeking" AND cat:cs.AI (34), sorted ascending, all read by title and abstract; read in full: Turner 2019, Turner and Tadepalli 2022, Krakovna and Kramar 2023, Tarsney 2025, Thorstad 2026, Miller et al. 2020. MIRI's Benson-Tilsen and Soares paper. Web search for discovery of Gallow, 'Instrumental Divergence' (retrieval refused). The Facebook discussion of October 2019 could not be retrieved (see retrievalFailures). _Earliest:_ Omohundro's 'The Nature of Self-Improving Artificial Intelligence' is the earliest located: dated 5 September 2007 (claimed) and read in its revision of 21 January 2008 (attested), which precedes 'The Basic AI Drives' (2008). The dataset's Yudkowsky records contesting Y19 by instrumental reasoning begin with an SL4 message of 3 October 2000 and include 'AI as a Positive and Negative Factor in Global Risk' (2006) and the Arbital 'Instrumental convergence' page (2015); they precede Omohundro and are linked, not re-recorded.
- **R10a** (goals-and-drives). Seed texts read: the Edge 'Myth of AI' responses (2014), Pinker 2015 and 2018, Hawkins 2015, Zador and LeCun 2019, Andreessen 2023; contesting: Omohundro 2008, Bostrom 2012, Bengio 2024. No arXiv search was run, since the proposition has no usual name; arXiv results of the R01 and R02 searches were checked for texts arguing benevolence, and none was found among their abstracts. Split from the R10 entry on 2026-10-29, when R10 was divided into R10a and R10b. _Earliest:_ Pinker's Edge response of 2014 is the earliest located among texts found; the Edge responses carry no dates of their own. The search did not reach earlier texts in 2000–2014 stating the proposition; the dataset's Yudkowsky records contesting Y02 from November 2000 show that the benevolence claim was argued on the SL4 list before then, in messages not recorded here. Earliest contesting located: Omohundro (2008).
- **R10b** (goals-and-drives). Seed texts read: the Edge 'Myth of AI' responses (2014), Pinker 2015, Zador and LeCun 2019, the 2019 thread (recorded by the debates group), Andreessen 2023, Belrose and Pope 2023; contesting: Bengio's comments in the 2019 thread, Russell 2019. LeCun's 'A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence' (2022) was not retrievable. No arXiv search was run, since the proposition has no usual name; arXiv results of the R01 and R02 searches were checked for texts arguing controllability, and none was found among their abstracts. Split from the R10 entry on 2026-10-29, when R10 was divided into R10a and R10b. _Earliest:_ Kelly's Edge response of 2014 is the earliest located among texts found; the Edge responses carry no dates of their own. Earliest contesting located: Bengio's comments in the 2019 thread (LessWrong copy of 4 October 2019).
- **R03** (misalignment). Seed texts read: Hubinger et al. RLO (dataset, LessWrong posts; not re-recorded), Koch, Langosco et al. arXiv 2105.14111 v1, Shah et al. 2210.01790 v1, Ngo 2209.00626 v1, Carlsmith 2311.08379 v1, Greenblatt et al. 2412.14093 v1, Hubinger et al. 2401.05566 v1, Meinke et al. 2412.04984 v1; Soares (MIRI blog, 2017) and Yudkowsky (MIRI blog, 2016) read for R06 and found to bear on R03. arXiv API, abstract search sorted by submission date ascending, first 50 results by title and abstract: "goal misgeneralization" (18 results), "inner alignment" (10, mostly astrophysics), "mesa-optimization"/"mesa-optimizer(s)" (7), "deceptive alignment"/"deceptively aligned" (20), "alignment faking" (33). Contesting texts: Belrose and Pope (optimists.ai, 2023, 2024), Turner (turntrout.com, 2024). LessWrong and the Alignment Forum refused this environment (HTTP 403), so DavidW (2023) and other posts there were not read. Web search used for discovery only. _Earliest:_ Soares's transcript of April 2017 is the earliest located text in this search that states that a trained optimiser's goal can match the training objective in training and diverge from it elsewhere. In the dataset, texts on the overlapping concepts B3 and B4 sit beside it: Arbital, Optimization daemons (2016-03-22, bears on B3), Christiano, What failure looks like (2019-03-17, earliest located on B3), Hubinger et al., RLO (2019, bears on B3 and earliest located use of the name there), Cotra (2021, earliest located on B4). No record here holds the earliest located use of a name: the dataset's RLO introduction (2019) uses "mesa-optimizer" and "inner alignment" before every text recorded here. Contesting: Belrose and Pope (November 2023) is the earliest located contesting text found.
- **R04** (misalignment). Seed texts on R03 read for passages on behaviour that changes once oversight is ineffective (Shah et al., Ngo, Carlsmith, Greenblatt et al.). arXiv API abstract search for "treacherous turn" (3 results, 2023–2026). Contesting texts searched among reviews of Bostrom's Superintelligence (Goertzel 2015, Davis 2014) and the essays of Belrose and Pope. Bostrom's Superintelligence itself is not accessible in full text from this environment. _Earliest:_ Ngo's report (August 2022) holds the earliest located role among this pack's R04 articulations only. The criterion is identical to the dataset's B1, where Yudkowsky's Creating Friendly AI (2001) is earliest located and Grace (2014), Soares et al. (2015), Arbital (2015), Hubinger et al. (2019) and Cotra (2021, 2022) articulate it; Christiano (2022) contests it there. Goertzel (2015) is the earliest located contesting text in this search, earlier than the dataset's contesting record.
- **R06** (misalignment). Seed texts read: the FLI open letter page and the research-priorities paper (AI Magazine version), Amodei et al. (dataset expression, arXiv v1), Yudkowsky (MIRI blog transcript, 2016), Soares (MIRI blog transcript, 2017), Ngo (2022), Hendrycks et al., Unsolved Problems in ML Safety (arXiv 2109.13916 v1; read, not recorded: it lists research problems without stating the criterion). arXiv API abstract searches "value alignment" AND cs.AI (first 50), "AI alignment" (first 50), "control problem" AND cs.AI (first 50, mostly control theory), "alignment problem" (484 results, almost all outside AI; first 50 read). Contesting texts: Davis (2014), Leike (2022), Belrose and Pope (2023), Millidge (2024). Barnett (2023), Wentworth (2020) and Pope (2023) are on LessWrong and were not retrievable. _Earliest:_ Russell's Edge response of 2014 (Of Myths and Moonshine), found by the same-family audit among the pack's sources, is the earliest located text that meets the criterion. Yudkowsky's transcript of December 2016 (talk of May 2016) was the earliest found by this group's own search that states that advanced AIs will not be aligned automatically and that alignment is hard; the FLI research-priorities paper (2015) and Amodei et al. (2016) are earlier but lack the not-by-default element and are recorded as bearing on it. The dataset's records on Y30 ("It will probably turn out fine") and Y28, Yudkowsky's texts from 2000 onward contesting those propositions, sit beside R06 and are not re-recorded; texts in other groups (Bostrom 2003, Omohundro 2008) may also bear on it. Davis's review of October 2014 is the earliest located contesting text. Leike (2022) is recorded as bearing on the proposition after the same-family audit; Pinker (2018) was moved here from R01.
- **R14** (misalignment). Seed texts read: Bender and Koller (ACL Anthology PDF), Shanahan (arXiv v1), Mitchell and Krakauer (arXiv v1), Andreas (arXiv v1), Piantadosi and Hill (arXiv v1). Added from the seed texts' references and discovery searches: Marcus (The Gradient, January 2020; Substack, June 2022), Browning and LeCun (Noema, 2022), janus (generative.ink, 2022), Mahowald et al. (arXiv v1, 2023), Bubeck et al. (arXiv v1, 2023), Kambhampati et al. (arXiv v1, 2024), Meinke et al. (arXiv v1, 2024). arXiv API abstract search "stochastic parrots" (23 results, from 2021). Bender, Gebru, McMillan-Major and Shmitchell (FAccT 2021) could not be retrieved (ACM HTTP 403; the Wikimedia Commons copy of the publisher PDF HTTP 429). _Earliest:_ Marcus's essay of 25 January 2020 is the earliest located text in this search that states that a large language model lacks understanding; Bender and Koller (July 2020) follows. Searle's Chinese room (1980) and other pre-2000 arguments about machine understanding are outside the bounds and not about language models. The dataset's Y09 and Y15 records are Yudkowsky's texts contesting related propositions (Y09 from 2022, Y15 from 2001) and are not re-recorded. Piantadosi and Hill (August 2022) is the earliest located contesting text found. Marcus (June 2022) is the earliest located use of "autocomplete" among the records; no record here uses "stochastic parrots" in a recorded passage, since the paper that names it could not be retrieved. Browning and LeCun (2022) and Kambhampati et al. (2024) are recorded as bearing on the proposition after the same-family audit.
- **R15** (openness-and-incentives). Seed texts read: Bostrom 2017 (openness); Mozilla joint statement (2023); Meta statement of support (July 2023); Zuckerberg 2024; Kapoor, Bommasani et al. 2024; Seger et al. 2023; NTIA report (July 2024); Andreessen 2023. LeCun: Senate Intelligence Committee testimony of 19 September 2023 refused (HTTP 403); his other statements on openness found were posts on X or press interviews, not used. Further texts found from seed texts' references and by web search for discovery: Goertzel and Pitt 2012 (JET); Alexander 2015; Brundage et al. 2018; Solaiman et al. 2019; EleutherAI 2021; Shevlane 2022; Narayanan and Kapoor 2023 and 2024 (AI Snake Oil); Llama 2 paper 2023; Howard 2023; Gopal et al. 2023; Hendrycks et al. 2023; Eiras et al. 2024; Bengio 2024; Ng, The Batch 257 (2024); Widder, Whittaker and West 2024. arXiv API: abs:"open source" AND abs:"model weights", and abs:"open-sourcing" AND abs:"misuse", each sorted by submission date ascending, first 50 results read by title (mostly unrelated software papers; Seger et al. was the relevant hit). _Earliest:_ Goertzel and Pitt (February 2012) is the earliest located text stating that open AGI development is probably preferable (safer) than closed; it concerns open-source AGI code, as no weights debate existed then. Bostrom 2017 and the OpenAI founding announcement of December 2015 (a dataset entity, not re-read: HTTP 403) are later. Earliest located contesting text: Alexander, 17 December 2015. No earlier text in the dataset on an overlapping concept (R15 has no dataset overlap).
- **R17** (openness-and-incentives). Seed texts read: Armstrong, Bostrom and Shulman 2013 (FHI report copy); Askell, Brundage and Hadfield 2019; Hendrycks 2023 (natural selection) and the AI-race section of Hendrycks et al. 2023; Narayanan and Kapoor 2025 (AI as Normal Technology) and their 2024 post on companies building products (read, not recorded: it concerns product-market fit, not safety). Also read: Hanson, Quillette 2023 and Overcoming Bias "AI Risk Again" (March 2023, not recorded: it concerns controlling organisations, not developer incentives); Liron, "Contra Hanson on AI Risk" (LessWrong, March 2023, read via the LessWrong API; not recorded, it does not address incentives); Bengio 2024; Hinton's Nobel banquet speech; Altman, "Machine intelligence, part 2" (2015, not recorded: it argues for regulation without addressing incentives); Andreessen 2023 (no passage on incentives producing safety). Web search for discovery: "AI companies commercial incentives safety market", Hanson 2023 posts. arXiv API: abs:"race" AND abs:"AI safety", and abs:"race to the bottom" AND cat:cs.CY, sorted ascending, first 50 read by title (later papers on racing, e.g. 2025–2026, not read in full). _Earliest:_ Among this pack's records, Hanson's Quillette essay (14 April 2023) is the earliest articulating text found. The dataset's economics collection (E04) already records Hanson's 2008 blog posts (Friendliness Factors, 16 November 2008, earliest located there) and Gans (2018, 2023); these precede it and are linked, not re-recorded. Earliest located contesting text: Armstrong, Bostrom and Shulman, FHI Technical Report 2013-1. Fewer articulating texts than contesting texts were found for R17: most published statements that markets or liability will make AI safe that the searches found were in press interviews or posts on X, which are not evidence.
- **R05** (takeoff). Seed texts read: Chalmers (2010, dataset copy), Kelly (2017), Brooks (2017), AI Impacts (2018) and Grace (2022), Drexler (2019), Aschenbrenner (2024), AI 2027 (2025), Narayanan and Kapoor (2025), Hanson's posts after 2013 (2014, 2019, 2022), Yudkowsky's MIRI posts of 2017. Chollet (2017) refused retrieval. Davidson's compute-centric takeoff report (2023) refused retrieval at Open Philanthropy and the Alignment Forum; his Forethought papers of 2025 (with Eth; with Houlden) were read instead. Bostrom (2002, 2003) read from caches shared with other groups. arXiv API, all fields, sorted by submission date ascending, first 50 results read by title and abstract: "intelligence explosion" (16 results), "fast takeoff" OR "hard takeoff" OR "slow takeoff" (1), "recursive self-improvement" (224; first 50), title "technological singularity" (10); read in full: Walsh 2016, Benthall 2017, Hutter 2012, Yampolskiy 2015, Sarma 2017, Whitfill and Wu 2025, MacAskill and Moorhouse 2025. Thorstad (2022) and Muehlhauser and Salamon (2012) located by web search for discovery and read in their primary PDFs. Web search used for discovery only (Hanson's post address). _Earliest:_ Bostrom (2003) meets the criterion (a transition from roughly human-level AI to superintelligence 'perhaps a matter of days rather than years') but is read only in an undated revised copy, so it does not hold the earliest-located role. Yudkowsky (20 October 2017) holds it, and meets the criterion only through the name “foom”, not through a stated period. Chalmers (2010) and Muehlhauser and Salamon (2012) are recorded as bearing on it, since neither states a period of hours to months. Bostrom (2002) was read and found to state a sudden transition only for uploads, conditionally. On the overlapping dataset concepts A4/A1, Yudkowsky's Creating Friendly AI (2001) and AI as a Positive and Negative Factor in Global Risk (2006) precede it in the period, and Good (1965) and Vinge (1993) precede the period; they are linked, not re-recorded. The earliest located contesting record within the pack is Hanson (2014); Hanson's What Core Argument? (2008) contests A4 in the dataset.
- **R13** (takeoff). Seed texts read: Marcus (2018, arXiv v1; 2022, Nautilus), Mitchell (2021, arXiv v1), Sutton (2019). Further texts located from the seed texts and by web search for discovery: Marcus (2024), Narayanan and Kapoor, 'AI scaling myths' (2024), Aschenbrenner (2024, chapter I), Gwern, 'The Scaling Hypothesis' (2020), Altman, 'The Intelligence Age' (2024), Bengio et al. (2023, arXiv v1) and Bengio (2024). No arXiv API search was run for R13's usual names ('hitting a wall', 'plateau', 'diminishing returns'), whose results would be dominated by technical papers on training dynamics; this is a gap. _Earliest:_ Marcus (2018) is the earliest text found that states that deep learning alone will not reach general intelligence. The dataset's Y13 records (Yudkowsky, 2000–2021) contest or bear on 'AGI is decades away' and are linked. Earlier statements that some approach to AI would fall short (Dreyfus, 1972, and others before 2000) are outside the period; Brooks (2017), Kelly (2017) and Walsh (2016) make related claims about distance to AGI but not about the limits of current approaches and were recorded on R05 or R16 instead. Earliest contesting located: Gwern, The Scaling Hypothesis (page dated 2020-05-28 to 2022-01-02; wording attested from 2022-01-02).
- **R16** (takeoff). Seed texts read: Christiano (2018, dataset address), Brooks (2017), Narayanan and Kapoor (2025), Drexler (2019), AI Impacts (2018), Grace (2022), Hanson (2014, 2022). Contesting texts located from the replies: Yudkowsky (2017, two posts). Kokotajlo, 'Against GDP as a metric for timelines and takeoff speeds' (LessWrong, 2020), and Davidson's takeoff report (2023) could not be retrieved. _Earliest:_ Brooks (September 2017) is the earliest articulation found; Christiano (February 2018) follows. The dataset's Y11 records (Yudkowsky, from 2000) are contesting or context records on a related concept, so no earlier articulation is linked. Hanson's posts (2008–2014) argue against a local explosion rather than stating a gradual transition in the criterion's terms; Hanson (2014) is recorded as contesting R05. The two contesting records are both Yudkowsky's, which leaves the risk side of R16 thin; the search found no other text that explicitly argues against a gradual takeoff.
- **balance** (takeoff). Count by position: risk side (articulating R05, contesting R13 or R16) 15 records; counter side (contesting R05, articulating R13 or R16) 17 records; bears_on 4 (two on R05, two on R13). The group paragraph asked for about 40 records; 36 were made, because three seed texts refused retrieval (Chollet 2017, Davidson 2023, Kokotajlo 2020) and several arXiv results were found not to meet the criteria. _Earliest:_ Not applicable.

## Omissions

- Russell, Marcus and Amodei written testimony (Senate Judiciary, May and July 2023) — judiciary.senate.gov refuses connections from this environment (HTTP 403); the printed hearing records on govinfo.gov were used instead. Marcus's and Russell's oral statements were read but not recorded: Marcus's concerns present harms without ranking them against catastrophic risk, and Russell's states the control problem without an extinction or disempowerment claim. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Munk Debate, 22 June 2023 (Bengio and Tegmark against LeCun and Mitchell) — No published transcript was located; video only. Gans's post on it is recorded for his own arguments. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Brundage et al., The Malicious Use of Artificial Intelligence (2018) — Read; it states no catastrophe-scale claim, so it neither meets R09 nor comes close enough for a bears_on record. Named in the R09 search log. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Ng's remarks to the US Senate AI Insight Forum (December 2023), Hinton's and LeCun's interviews and posts on X — Press reports and social-media posts are discovery only. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Bengio et al., "Managing extreme AI risks amid rapid progress" (Science, May 2024) — Not retrieved; the arXiv v1 text of October 2023 is recorded and its version noted. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Yudkowsky, Time essay of 29 March 2023 and other Yudkowsky texts — In the dataset on Yudkowsky collection codes; not re-recorded. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Comments by Francesca Rossi, Richard Mallah, Elliot Olds, Yuri Barzov and others in the 2019 thread — Outside the seed list; recorded participants are the four named in the copy's title who argue the propositions. (debates)
- The Munk Debate on AI risk (June 2023: Bengio and Tegmark against LeCun and Mitchell) — No published transcript was located; video only. (debates)
- LeCun–Bengio exchanges on X and in press interviews, 2023–2024 — Posts on X are outside the scope; interviews are reporting. (debates)
- Facebook discussion on instrumental convergence of October 2019 (LeCun, Russell, Bengio, Zador and others), LessWrong and Alignment Forum copies — Not retrieved by this group: both copies returned HTTP 403 and the GreaterWrong mirror reset the connection. Recorded instead from the LessWrong copy: the debates group retrieved the LessWrong post through the LessWrong GraphQL API (source pace-2019-instrumental-convergence-debate) and records the thread (debate facebook-2019-instrumental-convergence). The original Facebook thread is outside the scope and was not compared. (goals-and-drives)
- LeCun, 'A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence' (OpenReview, 2022) — OpenReview returned HTTP 403. No other expression by the author was located. (goals-and-drives)
- Armstrong, 'General purpose intelligence: arguing the orthogonality thesis' (Analysis and Metaphysics, 2013) — The FHI copy could not be reached (proxy refused the host); no other author copy located. (goals-and-drives)
- Gallow, 'Instrumental Divergence' (Philosophical Studies, 2024) — PhilArchive returned HTTP 403; the abstract was seen only in a search result, which is not evidence. (goals-and-drives)
- Totschnig, 'Fully Autonomous AI' (Science and Engineering Ethics, 2020) — The Springer PDF address returned a small HTML page, not the paper; not pursued. (goals-and-drives)
- Other Edge Reality Club responses of 2014 (Myhrvold, Gershenfeld, Shermer, Krauss and others) and other responses to the Edge 2015 Annual Question — Skimmed only; not recorded, to keep to the passages that most directly meet or dispute the criteria. (goals-and-drives)
- Carlsmith, "Is Power-Seeking AI an Existential Risk?" (2022) and Bengio's 2023 posts — Seeded for the catastrophe-and-priorities group; not recorded on R02 here to avoid overlap. A record of Carlsmith on R02 may be added if that group does not cover the instrumental-convergence premise. (goals-and-drives)
- Hadshar, "A Review of the Evidence for Existential Risk from AI via Misaligned Power-Seeking" (arXiv 2023), and later empirical arXiv papers on instrumental behaviour in language models (2025-2026) — Read by abstract only; reviews and evaluations of evidence rather than statements of the proposition. Not recorded. (goals-and-drives)
- Books: Bostrom, Superintelligence (2014); Russell, Human Compatible (2019); Pinker, Enlightenment Now (2018) — Not accessible in full; Russell 2019 and Pinker 2018 are recorded from the excerpts their publishers posted. (goals-and-drives)
- Hubinger, van Merwijk, Mikulik, Skalse and Garrabrant, Risks from Learned Optimization (arXiv 1906.01820, 2019) — The same work is in the dataset as LessWrong posts recorded on B3 (rlo-2019-introduction, rlo-2019-deceptive-alignment); linked, not re-recorded. (misalignment)
- Christiano, What failure looks like (2019); Cotra (2021, 2022); Hubinger (2022); Yudkowsky, AGI Ruin (2022) — Recorded in the dataset on B1, B3 or B4; linked through datasetLink. (misalignment)
- Bender, Gebru, McMillan-Major and Shmitchell, On the Dangers of Stochastic Parrots (FAccT 2021) — Not retrievable (see retrievalFailures); no secondary copy used. (misalignment)
- Hicks, Humphries and Slater, ChatGPT is bullshit (Ethics and Information Technology, 2024) — Springer served a browser challenge page instead of the PDF; not recorded. (misalignment)
- DavidW, Deceptive Alignment is <1% Likely by Default (LessWrong, 2023); Wentworth, Alignment By Default (2020); Barnett, Evaluating the historical value misspecification argument (2023); Pope, My Objections to "We're All Gonna Die with Eliezer Yudkowsky" (2023) — LessWrong and the Alignment Forum refused connections (HTTP 403); these counter-side texts are not recorded, which leaves R03, R04 and R06 with fewer contesting records than the literature holds. (misalignment)
- Hendrycks, Carlini, Schulman and Steinhardt, Unsolved Problems in ML Safety (2021) — Read; it presents research problems without stating that alignment is hard or will not be solved by default. Not recorded. (misalignment)
- Bostrom, Superintelligence (2014); Russell, Human Compatible (2019) — Books not accessible in full text from this environment; Davis (2014), Goertzel (2015) and Turner (2024) quote Bostrom, and those quotations are not used as evidence of Bostrom's text. (misalignment)
- Chomsky, Roberts and Watumull, The False Promise of ChatGPT (New York Times, 2023) — Paywalled; not retrieved. (misalignment)
- LeCun, A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence (2022) — Not retrieved: OpenReview returned HTTP 403 to the goals-and-drives group (see its retrieval failures), and the cached response is the error page; its passages on autoregressive LLMs, which could bear on R14, were not read. (misalignment)
- LeCun, written testimony to the Senate Select Committee on Intelligence, 19 September 2023 — Retrieval refused (HTTP 403) at both addresses found; LeCun's other statements on open models located were posts on X and press interviews, not usable as evidence. (openness-and-incentives)
- Ng, letters in The Batch on open source and extinction-risk claims (2023) — The letters of 7 June and 25 October 2023 on extinction-risk claims are recorded by the catastrophe-and-priorities group (R07, R11). On open models this group found no 2023 letter beyond press reports of his remarks, and records the July 2024 letter instead. (openness-and-incentives)
- Clement Delangue (Hugging Face), House Science Committee testimony, June 2023 — Not located by the search; not recorded. (openness-and-incentives)
- OpenAI, "Better Language Models and Their Implications" (February 2019) and "Introducing OpenAI" (December 2015) — openai.com refused retrieval (HTTP 403). The staged-release decision is recorded from the OpenAI report of August 2019 (Solaiman et al.). (openness-and-incentives)
- Bommasani et al., "Considerations for Governing Open Foundation Models" (Stanford HAI brief, 2023; Science, 2024) — Not retrieved; the fuller arXiv position paper by Kapoor, Bommasani et al. (2024) is recorded. (openness-and-incentives)
- International AI Safety Report (2025), section on open-weight models — Not read for this group; the catastrophe group retrieves the report. (openness-and-incentives)
- Hanson's 2008 posts and Gans (2018, 2023) — Recorded in the dataset on E04; linked from the R17 earliest-located record, not re-recorded. (openness-and-incentives)
- Chollet, "The impossibility of intelligence explosion" (Medium, 2017) — Medium returned HTTP 403. Defined as a document so that Yudkowsky's reply can name it; no record rests on it, and the passages Yudkowsky quotes were not used as evidence of Chollet's text. (takeoff)
- Davidson, "What a compute-centric framework says about takeoff speeds" (Open Philanthropy, 2023) — Open Philanthropy and the Alignment Forum and LessWrong copies returned HTTP 403. Davidson's Forethought papers of 2025 were recorded instead. (takeoff)
- Kokotajlo, "Against GDP as a metric for timelines and takeoff speeds" (LessWrong, 2020) — LessWrong returned HTTP 403; would have been a contesting record on R16. (takeoff)
- Hanson, "How Lumpy AI Services?" (Overcoming Bias, 2019-02-14) — Retrieved and read; most of the post quotes Drexler and others (relay), and its own anti-foom statement repeats Hanson (2014). Not recorded. (takeoff)
- Bostrom, "Existential Risks" (2002) — Read; its sudden-transition passage concerns uploads and is conditional ("If the process is sudden"); does not meet R05. (takeoff)
- Hutter, "Can Intelligence Explode?" (arXiv 2012) — Read in part; it analyses what an intelligence explosion would mean and states agreement with Chalmers, but no passage stating that it will occur within a short period was located in the pages read. Candidate for a bears_on record. (takeoff)
- Yampolskiy, "From Seed AI to Technological Singularity via Recursively Self-Improving Software" (arXiv 2015) — Read in part; it poses the hard/soft takeoff question and analyses limits on recursive self-improvement without stating a position in the passages read. (takeoff)
- Sarma, "Brief Notes on Hard Takeoff, Value Alignment, and Coherent Extrapolated Volition" (arXiv 2017) — Read in full; it argues that a hard takeoff would be preferable under correct design, without stating that one will occur. (takeoff)
- MacAskill and Moorhouse, "Preparing for the Intelligence Explosion" (Forethought, March 2025; arXiv 2506.14863) — Read in part; its central claim is a century of technological progress in a decade, not a period for the rise in AI capability as such; left for a later pass. (takeoff)
- Whitfill and Wu, "Will Compute Bottlenecks Prevent an Intelligence Explosion?" (arXiv 2025) — Abstract read; it reports divergent estimates and takes no position; not recorded. (takeoff)
- Sotala, "How feasible is the rapid development of artificial superintelligence?" (Physica Scripta, 2017) — The IOP address returned an HTML landing page, not the paper; not recorded. (takeoff)
- Bostrom, Superintelligence (2014); Kurzweil, The Singularity Is Near (2005) — Books not legally accessible in full text from this environment (scope rule). Hanson (2014), Benthall (2017), Walsh (2016) and Thorstad (2022) answer Superintelligence; no reply item is made because the book is not a dataset or pack document. (takeoff)
- Hanson's and Yudkowsky's posts of 2008–2013 and Christiano–Yudkowsky (2021) — In the dataset on A4/A1/Y11; linked in debates and datasetLink, not re-recorded. (takeoff)
- Press reports and interviews (for example of LeCun, Altman, Hassabis on scaling) — Discovery only under the scope rules. (takeoff)

## Open questions from the drafts

- Should the earliest-located role on R12 rest on Crawford and Calo (Nature, 2016), read only through its standfirst (a relay), or on the DAIR statement (2023), the earliest text read in the authors' own words? _Recommended:_ Keep Crawford and Calo provisionally; read the full Nature comment (library access or author copy) before admission, and move the role to DAIR if the body does not state the proposition. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Bostrom (2002) section 6.3 is recorded as articulating R09 for its scenario of a small group entrenching its values through the first superintelligence. Does that meet "deliberate use … to seize or entrench power" closely enough, or should it be a bears_on record, with Marcus (2023) as the earliest located? _Recommended:_ Cross-family check Part A should test this directly. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- Several pages (Bengio's three posts, Bender and Hanna) show later modification dates or revised wording; no earlier copy could be compared because the Internet Archive is unreachable. _Recommended:_ Compare against archived copies from another environment before admission. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- The International AI Safety Report is recorded as bears_on R08 because it reports expert disagreement rather than asserting the proposition. Should reports of this kind sit in the pack at all, or only as context in a debate note? _Recommended:_ Keep as bears_on; it documents the state of disagreement with a date. (catastrophe-and-priorities)
- The 2019 thread is read in a compiler's copy that states it fixed typos and formatting, and its comments are undated. Should its records be dated by the copy (2019-10-04) with the wording attested from it, as here? _Recommended:_ Yes; and compare the Facebook original if a later pass can retrieve it. (debates)
- Bostrom 2003 states R01 in a revised version whose date is unknown. Should a comparison with the 2003 printed proceedings be sought, so that it could become the earliest located text on R01 in place of Bostrom 2012? _Recommended:_ Keep Bostrom 2012 as earliest located until the 2003 printed wording is compared; record the correction if it matches. (goals-and-drives)
- R10 earliest located rests on page order within an undated Edge discussion (Pinker before Kelly). Is page order an acceptable basis? _Recommended:_ Accept with the stated qualification, or drop the role and mark both 2014 responses as articulations with a note. Overtaken on 2026-10-29: after the split, Pinker's response is recorded on R10a and Kelly's on R10b, so page order no longer decides an earliest-located role. (goals-and-drives)
- The Facebook discussion of October 2019 is a seed debate but could not be retrieved. Should a later session retrieve the LessWrong copy by another permitted route? _Recommended:_ Retry from an environment where LessWrong is reachable; record it as a later copy (attested at its LessWrong posting date) with replies among LeCun, Russell, Bengio and Zador. Done on 2026-10-29: the debates group retrieved the LessWrong post through the LessWrong GraphQL API (source pace-2019-instrumental-convergence-debate) and records the thread (debate facebook-2019-instrumental-convergence). (goals-and-drives)
- Benson-Tilsen and Soares is dated only by PDF file metadata (November 2015). Is that sufficient? _Recommended:_ Seek a dated proceedings or MIRI listing; until then keep the date with its basis visible. (goals-and-drives)
- Pinker 2014 and Zador and LeCun 2019 state that intelligence and goals are separate, which is the independence R01 asserts, while arguing R10a. Should they also be recorded as bearing on R01? _Recommended:_ No separate record; the qualification on each R10a record names the point. (goals-and-drives)
- R03's criterion includes a system that pursues an objective other than the one 'its developers intended'. Read literally, it admits outer misspecification (the smiles example in Yudkowsky's 2016 transcript; Arbital, Context disaster, 2015, and Creating Friendly AI, 2001, in the dataset), which would move the earliest located text before Soares (2017). _Recommended:_ Narrow the criterion to an objective other than the one training specified, in line with the label 'inner', or keep it and add the outer-misspecification texts as a separate earliest-located finding; decide before admission. (misalignment)
- R04 is criterion-identical to B1. Ngo (2022) holds the earliest located role only within this pack. _Recommended:_ Admit the R04 records onto B1 as the scope note recommends; then Ngo's report becomes an articulation and the earliest-located role stays with Creating Friendly AI (2001). (misalignment)
- Several records rest on current copies of posts that state later updates (Turner, updated 2026-08-19) or carry an inconsistent date (Goertzel's page gives November and December 2015; the FLI letter page gives 28 October 2015 for a letter released in January 2015). _Recommended:_ Keep the attested and claimed dates as recorded; a reviewer with archive access could compare the earliest captures. (misalignment)
- Counter-side texts on R03, R04 and R06 are concentrated in four authors (Belrose, Pope, Turner, Millidge) plus Davis, Goertzel and Leike, because LessWrong was not retrievable. _Recommended:_ Retrieve the LessWrong posts listed in omissions from an environment that can reach the site, and add them before admission. (misalignment)
- Ngo's report changed authors and text between v1 (Ngo alone, August 2022) and later versions (with Chan and Mindermann). _Recommended:_ Keep the v1 record; record a later version separately only if its wording is cited. (misalignment)
- Several sources here are also read by other groups (Hendrycks et al. 2023 overview, Bengio 2024, Hinton's banquet speech, Narayanan and Kapoor 2025, Andreessen 2023). Their pack source keys and document ids may differ between drafts, and the per-source quotation budget is shared. _Recommended:_ At merge, unify keys and document ids for the same expression and re-run the per-source quotation check across all drafts. (openness-and-incentives)
- Goertzel and Pitt (2012) concern open-source AGI code and development, not model weights. Should a text predating the weights debate hold earliest-located on R15? _Recommended:_ Keep it, with the qualification as written; the criterion's "open source" term covers it, and the next located texts (EleutherAI 2021) are about weights. (openness-and-incentives)
- R17 has two articulating records against seven contesting ones, because the dataset's E04 already holds the economics texts and the other statements found were press interviews or posts on X. _Recommended:_ Record the imbalance in the review note rather than admit weaker texts; consider a targeted search of law-and-economics and policy writing (liability, tort) for 2023–2026. (openness-and-incentives)
- Andreessen (2023), NTIA (2024) and Ng (2024) are recorded as bears_on R15. A reviewer may judge Andreessen's "cat is out of the bag" and defensive-use argument to state that open release adds little marginal risk. _Recommended:_ Keep as bears_on unless the reviewer finds a passage stating the marginal-risk claim directly. (openness-and-incentives)
- R05 overlaps A4/A1. Should these records be admitted onto A4 (fast or hard takeoff) and A1 (intelligence explosion) or onto a concept of their own? _Recommended:_ Admit onto A4 as the overlap note recommends, keeping the earliest-located role scoped to texts outside the dataset's A4 records, since Creating Friendly AI (2001) already precedes Bostrom (2003) on A4. (takeoff)
- Several documents may be defined by more than one group: bostrom-2003-ethical-issues (goals-and-drives), altman-2015-machine-intelligence-part-1 (catastrophe-and-priorities), bengio-2023-managing-extreme-risks and bengio-2024-reasoning-arguments (catastrophe-and-priorities), narayanan-kapoor and Aschenbrenner texts (openness-and-incentives). _Recommended:_ Reconcile ids and documentWords at merge; this draft uses the other groups' document ids and cache keys where they were visible, and its own cache key for the Bengio et al. arXiv v1. (takeoff)
- This pass re-fetched arXiv 2310.17688v1 into the shared cache key bengio-2023-managing-extreme-risks before switching to its own key, overwriting whatever file another group had cached under that key. _Recommended:_ The catastrophe-and-priorities group should re-fetch its Bengio et al. source and re-run its check if it used a different expression under that key. Checked 2026-10-29: both cache files hold the same bytes (SHA-256 d6ec9c8b…), and the two source entries are merged into bengio-2023-managing-ai-risks. (takeoff)
- R16's risk side has only two records, both by Yudkowsky. Is a targeted search of LessWrong and the Alignment Forum (blocked here) needed to balance it? _Recommended:_ Retrieve Kokotajlo (2020) and Hanson's LessWrong reply to Yudkowsky's AlphaGo Zero post (2017) by another route in a later pass. (takeoff)
- The R13 criterion joins a claim about limits of current approaches with the consequence that catastrophe-capable AI is not near; Marcus (2018, 2022) and Narayanan and Kapoor (2024) state the first part only. _Recommended:_ Keep them as articulations with qualifications, or reword the criterion so that the second clause is a consequence rather than a requirement. Resolved on 2026-10-29: the consequence clause was removed from the R13 criterion. (takeoff)
